Britain’s ambassador to the United States has urged England fans to remain on their best behaviour as tensions mount ahead of the World Cup semi-final against Argentina.

Sir Christian Turner called on travelling supporters in Atlanta to continue to act as “ambassadors for the country” amid fears that the historic dispute over the Falkland Islands could spill into violence.

The FBI has designated Wednesday night’s match “high risk”, making it the most serious security concern of the tournament so far.

“Continue to support each other, whatever the outcome,” Sir Chris told The Telegraph. “Enjoy the moment, enjoy the occasion, enjoy the hospitality. It’s a game of football.”

Tensions have been inflamed by recent claims from the Argentine government that Britain is an “usurping” power and that the Falkland Islanders were “artificially implanted”.

So far, England’s games in the tournament have seen little to no violence.

Of the estimated 50,000 fans who have made the journey to the United States for the World Cup, just 25 needed assistance from the embassy for issues like lost passports, medical problems, or visa help.

“It’s a wonderful sign of how the fans have been ambassadors for the country,” Sir Chris added.

Thomas Tuchel has twice picked Jude Bellingham and Morgan Rogers in the same starting line-up. In the group stages against Panama, Bellingham had a deeper role in midfield and Rogers was the No10. The only other time they have started was Tuchel’s second match in charge, when he fielded the childhood pals in the win over Latvia.

Tuchel has never picked a back four of Reece James, John Stones, Marc Guéhi and Djed Spence during his time in charge of England.

John Byers is – and I’m sure he won’t mind me saying it – one of the older England fans at this World Cup. He’s 68 now and was eight when England won the thing. He remembers it well. He made a banner out of toilet paper, grabbed his mum’s red lipstick and scrawled E-N-G-L-A-N-D on it, a letter on each piece of loo roll. “This is our best chance to beat Argentina,” he says, “They haven’t been too good so far. We’ve got the better team.”

John, who worked in construction, retired four weeks ago especially in time for the World Cup. He’s from Birmingham and used to go and see Jude Bellingham playing in the Birmingham City youth team. “ I watched Jude as a nine-year-old and I used to tell everybody this guy is a diamond. I was right.”

Morgan Rogers deserves his chance for England and it makes sense to use him on the right, behind Harry Kane, which has become a problem position with Bukayo Saka not fully fit and Noni Madueke struggling. Rogers has made an impact from the bench and also did well when he started against Panama. Crucially he is also fairly fresh and raring to go. Feels like it could be a big game ahead for him alongside his friend, Jude Bellingham.

Giuliano Simeone, the youngest of El Cholo, Diego Simeone’s three footballing sons, replaces Rodrigo De Paul on the right of midfield:

Reece James is an excellent full-back, offering more than Ezri Konsa, a centre-half, in terms of crossing ability and as an accomplished midfielder, he is comfortable moving into the inside-right channel. Nico O’Reilly usually does that job on the left and maybe Tuchel is sacrificing his ability to ghost into attacking positions for Spence’s thrust and greater tackling?  As for Rogers, Saka is not fit to play much more than an hour and Tuchel obviously thinks he is better used against a tiring defence than using him to set the tone.

Rogers often plays wide for Villa and, although he will not drive to the byline as often as Madueke, his delivery will be more reliable.

It’s all been super good natured in and around the stadium. So far at least. Not least because there are just tens of thousands of Argentinian fans, hugely outnumbering England supporters. We’ll see what it’s like at kick-off inside the stadium but I expect this to be another England away game. And we did OK in the Azteca in Mexico with the crowd against us.

Officials tell me that they anticipate – or at least are most worried – if Argentina lose. While England’s animus is sporting (Hand of God in 1986 and the Beckham kick in 1998), theirs is political. Obviously the vast majority of fans on both sides are fantastically well-behaved but there are big worries from a security point of view if Argentina go out today.

It seems there has been a surprise… we’re just getting word that it looks to be Reece James at right-back. That means Tuchel is still yet to name the same back four in successive games at the World Cup. In for Konsa. Djed Spence is in at left-back too…

Wow! That’s two surprises expected in Tuchel’s line-up as we approach official confirmation of the teams.

The thought had been that the England head coach could stick with the same back four. But it appears he is going with Reece James at right-back, in for Ezri Konsa, and Djed Spence at left-back, ahead of Nico O’Reilly. That means the probable team is:

Pickford; James, Stones, Guehi, Spence; Rice, Anderson; Rogers, Bellingham, Gordon; Kane.

Argentina have played four and won four, two of them on penalties, though they did not have to when going through to their first final at home in 1978, a tournament of two group stages. The hosts fortuitously defeated Peru 6-0 in the last of the second-round group games, scoring three more goals than they needed to pip Brazil on goal difference,.

A man who scored twice in that match and two more in the final, Mario Kempes, celebrates his 72nd birthday today.

England, by contrast  have played in three semi-final, beating Portugal in 1966 and losing to West Germany in 1990 and Croatia in 2018.

The only grumblings I’ve seen among Argentine journalists focus on the full-back positions with advocates pushing for Nicolás González (Atlético), essentially a winger who has come on for Tagliafico in the last two games when Argentina were behind against Cape Verde and level with Switzerland. Tagliafico, who played in the 2022 final, is good enough going forward and offers more in defence, though he isn’t Giacinto Facchetti.

Gonzalo Montiel (River Plate), who is the reserve right-back, came on for Molina in the 2022 quarter-final and finals, scoring in both penalty shoot-outs.

Unless Thomas Tuchel springs a surprise, the expectation is that Rogers is likely to be the only change. The Argentine flags have maps of the Falkland Islands on them, which should be in breach of strict Fifa rules that prohibit political expression inside the stadiums.

I’m at the ground now and it’s wall to wall Argentina fans with a smattering of England supporters. All super peaceful from everything I’ve seen.

But the major worry, I’m told, from a law enforcement point of view, is an Argentina defeat. Most of the United States seems to want an England win. Except maybe the Atlanta police department.

A whopping 76 per cent of Telegraph readers are adamant England will overcome Argentina and book their place in Sunday’s final...

For the first time since the 1986 World Cup, Liverpool do not have a single representative in an England squad for a major tournament.

It is therefore understandable, perhaps, why their social media post previewing Alexis Mac Allister’s involvement for Argentina in tonight’s semi-final has not been well received by a handful of England fans online.

“Such a disgrace posting this. And I’m a Liverpool fan,” read one reply.

Another fan wrote: “Imagine doing those emojis on a post about the opponents of your home nation.”

It’s important to point out that Liverpool published similar posts in support of players contracted to the club earlier in the tournament.

Telegraph Sport’s Gavin Mairs is behind enemy lines ahead of England rugby’s Nations Championship Test against Argentina on Saturday...

The skies are grey over Buenos Aires, but the city is already resplendent in La Albiceleste colours.

Dawn was just breaking this morning when I spotted a homeless gentleman walking along the walkway beside the docks in Puerto Madero wearing an Argentina top.

The lady serving in the bakery near Plaza de Mayo, where the official seat of government is located in Casa Rosada (the Pink House), was wearing her football top under work clothes, so she could make a quick get-away to the official fan festival in the Palermo district.

By mid-morning, the city was a sea of white and sky blue. In contrast, I have seen just one England supporter brave enough to don his football jersey and even he was having second thoughts about keeping it on.

Mike McGrath stole this from some Argentina fans in our hotel lobby – it’s coming home! Or he is….

Ian Wright has attracted criticism from Falklands veterans after predicting England’s World Cup semi-final against Argentina will be a “war”.

The former Arsenal and England striker was previewing tonight’s tie as part of ITV’s coverage of Spain’s victory over France on Tuesday evening.

“There’s going to be chaos in the game; there’s going to be chaos in the stands; they’re not separating the fans. It’s a game where we’re going to have to show a lot of composure,” said Wright, who won 33 caps for England.

“It’s a country that, as time’s gone by, I’ve learnt a lot about them, and I have no love for them. Obviously, [Diego] Maradona’s somebody that’s done some amazing things I’ve seen.

“It’s a team that we have to beat and it’s going to be a tough one; it’s going to be a real tough one, a real attritional war we’re going into, and we have to recognise that.

“I’ve heard Thomas Tuchel saying it’s just a game. It’s not just a game, and they’ll realise it’s not just a game. It’s going to be more than that when they play.”

255 British soldiers lost their lives during the 1982 Falklands War. Simon Weston, who suffered severe burns during the Argentine bombing of the Galahad Ship, condemned the comparison between a football match and war.

“Some people describe a footballer as a hero on the field but that’s completely misusing the language,” he told The Telegraph. “They’re footballers. It’s hardly like they’re risking their lives on the pitch. It’s nothing like a battlefield.”

Marc Guéhi has claimed there is “no pressure at all” on England ahead of their semi-final against Argentina.

The England centre-back said the onus was on Argentina to win the game as World Cup holders.

“What’s the pressure? The onus is on them, they are the world champions,” Guéhi said.

“They need to come out and defend their title so there is no pressure on us at all.”

🗣️ "What's the pressure? The onus is on them. They're the world champions. They need to come out and defend their title."England defender Marc Guehi says the pressure is on Argentina ahead of the World Cup semi-final 🏴󠁧󠁢󠁥󠁮󠁧󠁿 pic.twitter.com/Ffu3YAojyD

The Manchester City defender was a second-half substitute in England’s tournament opener against Croatia but has started every game since then, including the entirety of the extra-time victory over Norway in the previous round.

“Both sides have big players. When you have that, you know it’s going to be a good game. It’s going to be a fun watch,” he added.

“It’s one you’re going to be talking about for a long time, so we’re excited.”

Remarkable flag-waving scenes in Atlanta, where Argentina fans have been making their presence felt...

BANDERAZO ARGENTINO EN ATLANTA 🇦🇷@Quilmes_Cerveza 🍻 pic.twitter.com/lLygS7ybAp

England’s rugby players will resist the temptation to wear their national team training kit out in public in Buenos Aires in the build-up to their Nations Championship Test against Argentina on Saturday.

Steve Borthwick’s side are scheduled to arrive in the Argentine capital on Thursday and are braced for a hostile reception should their footballing counterparts reach the World Cup final with victory in Atlanta this evening.

Alex Coles, the England second row, said: “I think, bearing in mind where we are and who we’re playing with, we’ve been pretty sensible about not wearing our English shirts around too much.”

Tommy Freeman, Coles’s England and Northampton team-mate, added: “We’ll see how hostile it gets. It’s big for Argentina and us and being here it’s heightened massively.”

1 - Tonight is 🇦🇷 Lionel Messi's first ever match against England.In the UEFA Champions League, Messi faced English teams 36 times and was involved in 33 goals (27 goals, 6 assists), a record for a player against clubs of one nation.Acquainted. pic.twitter.com/HUnhFpZYvb

Thomas Tuchel has produced a reassuringly indifferent response to the news that Argentina will wear their “lucky” kit tonight.

The holders will swap their traditional sky blue and white stripes for their navy blue away strip. England, as the designated home side in the tie, will wear their all-white home kit.

Argentina previously wore dark blue in World Cup victories over the English in 1986, when Diego Maradona scored his infamous “Hand of God” goal, and again in 1998, when England were eliminated on penalties in the round of 16.

“I would have done the same if there ⁠was any superstition combined with it,” said Tuchel. “So credit to them. I was not aware of that.”

The German also admitted he had his own superstitions which help him manage stress level before a big game.

“I have my superstitious routines. ​I will not tell you because another superstition ‌is that if I tell you, it will not work,” he said.

“We have routines that keep you grounded and calm through the day, and that will not change. We have, of course, also ‌our ​lucky charms, and these things ⁠are just normal in high-level sport.”

"I would have done the same." 😅Thomas Tuchel is asked about claims that Argentina are wearing their 'lucky' blue shirt against England 🤔 pic.twitter.com/GkjPQqBsAd

Sir Keir Starmer has used his final appearance at Prime Minister’s Questions to wish England well in tonight’s World Cup semi-final.

Having been compared to Jude Bellingham by Graham Stewart, the Conservative MP for Beverley and Holderness, Starmer told the House of Commons: “For the England team, as with my successor [Andy Burnham], I won’t give advice, I will simply give my wholehearted support.

“And Mr Speaker, just for the record, I don’t care what the score is tonight as long as we win.”

🚨 WATCH: Keir Starmer jokes about tonight's England vs Argentina match in his final PMQs "This morning I had meetings with Ministerial colleagues and others. In addition to my duties in this House, I have an important appointment with the television at 8pm" #PMQs pic.twitter.com/UVP7n8dpTY

We’re still around five hours from any official announcement on team news, but the early indications are that Thomas Tuchel is considering retaining the back line that started England’s extra-time victory over Norway.

Tuchel deployed a back four containing Ezri Konsa, John Stones, Marc Guehi and Nico O’Reilly in the previous round before introducing Reece James, Djed Spence and Dan Burn off the bench.

James, the Chelsea right-back, is available to start from the off in Atlanta after recovering from a hamstring injury he picked up in the goalless draw with Ghana.

Tuchel opted to field Konsa at right-back in the absence of James and the suspended Jarell Quansah and could stick with the Aston Villa defender after impressing against Erling Haaland and Co.

Elsewhere, Declan Rice is likely to keep his starting place in midfield. The Arsenal midfielder was withdrawn at half-time against Norway after being struck down with a stomach bug in the build-up to the game but has sufficiently recovered to be considered for selection this evening.

The other dilemma facing Tuchel is who to start on the right side of attack. Bukayo Saka impressed after replacing Noni Madueke against Norway, but there have been previous concerns over a chronic Achilles issue.

Tuchel also has the option of bringing in Marcus Rashford and playing him on the right or switching Anthony Gordon from the left. Morgan Rogers, the Aston Villa playmaker is another option on the right side of attack after he made an impressive contribution off the bench in the quarter-finals.

Sam Wallace, Telegraph Sport’s chief football writer, summaries the significance of tonight’s tie and explains why it could represent Lionel Messi’s final game in international football.

📅 1962, 1966, 1986, 1998, 2002... now, 2026.Tomorrow's semi-final will mark the sixth time England has faced Argentina at a World Cup.The Three Lions beat their South American foes three out of those five times. Can Tuchel's squad win a fourth match?@SamWallaceTel breaks… pic.twitter.com/fx0tbjgGdl

Wayne Rooney, the former England forward, has made another outlandish claim which I hope doesn’t come back to bite him.

Less than a week after he promised to row down the River Mersey if Norway beat Brazil to reach the World Cup quarter-finals, Rooney has made an equally bullish prediction for tonight’s semi-final...

"If Harry Kane is on it, he'll destroy these two centre-backs" 🏴󠁧󠁢󠁥󠁮󠁧󠁿 🇦🇷 pic.twitter.com/9vqdSouHMJ

Police in Atlanta, Georgia, have been forced to intervene after rival sets of Argentina fans were involved in violent clashes on the eve of tonight’s semi-final.

Glass bottles, tables and chairs were thrown in an incident involving supporters of Argentine clubs San Lorenzo and Huracan.

Argentina supporters were asked to gather for a “flag-waving rally” the night before their nation’s last-four tie against England but the event descended into chaos when two ultra groups started fighting and caused many to flee for their safety.

With a place in the World Cup final on the line, Argentina supporters are resorting to bizarre methods in a bid to curse England’s hopes of progressing.

One fan told Reuters: “During this World Cup, when my friends and I get get together, we freeze the players. We put the star player and the goalkeeper on the same piece of paper and freeze in it in the freezer.”

⚽ Argentina fans in Buenos Aires perform pre-match rituals such as freezing stickers of rival players and wearing lucky jerseys ahead of the World Cup semi-final against England https://t.co/HcoxGvDyvH pic.twitter.com/YncKZOupLS

For those requiring a detailed history on the acrimony at the heart of this storied rivalry, look no further than Sam Wallace’s excellent essay, which explains why this tie will always be more than just a football match.

Here’s a snippet of Sam on the fixture’s most infamous chapter: the 1986 World Cup quarter-final, which took place four years after the Falklands War.

“You can’t grasp what it all means to the people back home, but something tells you it is the greatest experience you will have as a player.” Gary Lineker, with a hat-trick against Poland and two more goals in the last-16 win over Paraguay, had assessed the mood correctly before England’s match against Argentina at the Estadio Azteca. But even he could not have guessed what was to come.

The war between Britain and Argentina, just four years earlier, hung heavily over the game. “I am a football manager, not a politician,” Bobby Robson, the England manager, said: “Don’t ask me that sort of question.”

Midfielder Glenn Hoddle warned that Maradona had learned from his experiences at the 1982 World Cup and “really come on strong”. He pondered whether England should man-mark Maradona and suggested left-back Kenny Sansom. “I don’t think we should change the way we play because of him,” Hoddle said. “You would have to re-shape the whole side.”

The Argentine players gave each of the England team a present before the game. Maradona punched the ball into the England goal in the 51st minute. His brilliant second was four minutes later. Lineker scored his sixth of the tournament and England lost 2-1.

In just over 10 hours time we will know the identity of Spain’s World Cup final opponents after the European champions dispatched 2018 winners and pre-tournament favourites France on Tuesday evening.

Immortality beckons for England, who are braced for their biggest match in a generation with familiar foes Argentina standing between them and a first World Cup final since 1966.

This is a fixture steeped in history. The two nations are meeting at a World Cup for the sixth time, renewing a bitter rivalry that has produced some of the tournament’s most memorable moments and enduring controversies.

Thomas Tuchel’s side have reached the last four without being entirely convincing. They needed extra time to beat Norway in the quarter-finals after surviving for over half an hour with 10 men against co-hosts Mexico in the previous round.

England’s fortunes have been intrinsically linked to the performances of Harry Kane and Jude Bellingham, who have scored six goals apiece to keep Tuchel’s side alive at this tournament.

Argentina’s route to the latter stages has been just as arduous. The defending champions needed extra time to overcome both Cape Verde and Switzerland, while they came from 2-0 down with 11 minutes remaining to beat Egypt and avoid one of the biggest shocks in World Cup history.

The holders remain on course to become only the third side after Italy (1934, 1938) and Brazil (1958, 1962) to retain their crown and in the process give Lionel Messi the fairy-tale end to a remarkable international career.

The 39-year-old has scored eight goals in the tournament and remains central to everything to Argentina’s fortunes. Remarkably, despite a career spanning more than two decades, tonight’s semi-final meeting will be Messi’s first ever international appearance against England.

Tuchel has spoken openly about the scale of the challenge facing his players. Stopping Messi has become one of football’s great unsolved problems, but the 52-year-old German insists his team must focus on imposing their own game plan rather than being consumed with the

Rarely has so much been at stake for both nations. For England, it is another chance to move one step closer to ending a wait that has now stretched almost 60 years. For Argentina, it is an opportunity to go level with Italy and Germany for the second-most World Cup titles (four). Either way, one of football’s most storied rivalries is about to add another chapter.

#2313. Il Parma rialza la testa, Gilardino: "Dai momenti difficili abbiamo creato qualcosa di forte"

Alberto Gilardino, tecnico del Parma, è intervenuto ai microfoni di Sky per commentare il successo dei ducali con un netto 0-3 sul campo dell'Atalanta. Ecco le sue parole:

"Di sicuro 0-3 non ci avrei nemmeno pensato, ma di fare una partita importante sì perché i ragazzi se lo meritavano. E così è stato. Abbiamo sofferto i primi 25' la veemenza dell'Atalanta, poi abbiamo segnato e ci siamo sbloccati. Fase difensiva incredibile stasera, poi bravi sulle ripartenze a trovare il secondo e terzo gol. I ragazzi si meritano questa vittoria, venivamo da settimane con discrete prestazioni ma senza risultati e brutte sconfitte. Dai momenti difficili abbiamo comunque creato qualcosa di forte e si è visto".

Vittoria della svolta? "Sia la partita con l'Udinese che stasera sono due clean sheet, è linfa, energia, positività. Ora 4 giorni per prepararci per il derby contro il Bologna. Ci serviva questa serata".

Cosa porta Gilardino al Parma? "Non mi spaventano i momenti di difficoltà, questo è un aspetto importante. Sapevo che venendo al Parma ci potevano essere anche momemti di difficoltà. Le esperienze passate mi hanno fatto crescere, maturare. Ho lavorato anche su me stesso, cerco di mantenere sempre un grande equilibrio, è un aspetto che cerco di portare nel gruppo. Sono felice per questi ragazzi, ricordando che siamo tra le squadre più giovani del campionato. La positività è energia e dominio incredibile e importante".

Sul secondo gol: coraggio di giocare in un momento delicato per uscire dalla pressione "Avere un giocatore come Touré davanti ci prospettava una partita così: attacco della profondità con Fabbian e Lontani ad attaccare il fuorigioco. Siamo stati bravi dopo i 25 minuti, dove potevamo fare meglio. Ci siamo messi a posto e abbiamo lavorato molto bene".

#2310. Giordano: “Abbiamo preso un gol da polli, ma la reazione è stata forte”

Bruno Giordano esalta il carattere della Lazio, il trascinatore Isaksen e i cambi.

La Lazio si gode il primato in classifica e Bruno Giordano intervenuto ai microfoni di Radiosei, analizza questo momento d'oro biancoceleste. Secondo l'ex bomber, la squadra sa vincere e soffrire grazie alla crescita della personalità  di Isaksen. Nonostante la stanchezza e i problemi tattici, la maturità mentale e i cambi azzeccati di Gattuso fanno la differenza.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Vittoria meritata. Non si può pretendere di giocare sempre come a Torino o a Reggio Emilia. La partita si era messa male, abbiamo preso un gol da polli, ma la reazione è stata forte; la squadra si è aggrappata ad Isaksen, che in questo momento sta trascinando il gruppo. Squadra un po' stanca che appunto si è aggrappata a lui, va sfruttato il suo momento. Il risultato assume anche più valore in questi casi, quando non tutto va al 100%.  Isaksen sta crescendo anche in personalità. Sotto l'aspetto del gioco non mi meraviglia, lo abbiamo visto in Europa contro di noi, i suoi colpi li ha. Ora sta prendendo sicurezza, i compagni lo cercano e questo lui lo avverte e gli dà tranquillità. Il gol di ieri è difficile e bellissimo, lui spesso li fa belli; mi ricordo poche reti sue brutte. Il modo di giocare e la fiducia del tecnico stanno facendo la differenza. Ho sempre detto che non sapeva gestire i 90', faceva mezz'ora bene poi calava, ora invece arriva alla fine come se fosse il 70' e questo è un valore aggiunto.

Taylor è un giocatore dinamico, tecnico, completo. L'uscita di Fadera è stata un bene per noi, ci stava mettendo in difficoltà; è stato un vantaggio che abbiamo saputo sfruttare.  Serve la maturità per capire che non tutte le partite posso andare allo stesso modo, c'è stata pazienza ieri e non è facile perché a volte ti puoi innervosire. La Lazio mi ha dato l'impressione che sta maturando anche sotto questo suo aspetto, ha la consapevolezza che anche se va in svantaggio può poi vincere.  I cambi della Lazio? Si vedeva che alcuni calciatori, soprattutto quelli sostituiti, erano stanchi. L'allenatore deve fare delle scelte e mettere a disposizione i migliori del momento. Tavares era un po' affaticato rispetto alle gare precedenti, Frattesi giocava troppo vicino ai difensori centrali del Cagliari e così mancava qualcosa in mezzo al campo. Ci può stare, rientra tutto nella logica delle cose.

#2308. Inter, due pericoli col Feyenoord: macchina da gol in Olanda ma in Champions...

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Si avvicina una nuova tappa del cammino europeo dell'Inter: i nerazzurri saranno impegnati a Rotterdam contro il Feyenoord martedì 3 novembre alle ore 21:00.

La sfida tra la squadra di Chivu e quella di van Bronckhorst, valida per la quarta giornata di League Phase della Champions League 2026/27, vedrà affrontarsi due squadre appaiate nella classifica generale a quota 3 punti dopo tre partite giocate.

L'Inter, dopo le due sconfitte contro Real Madrid e Club Brugge, ha battuto lo Shakhtar a San Siro grazie alla rete di Carlos Augusto, mentre gli olandesi hanno perso nelle due trasferte disputate a Barcellona (5-1) e a Siviglia contro il Betis (1-0), trovando la vittoria al "De Kuip" contro il Como, sconfitto 3-2.

Il Feyenoord ha iniziato la stagione in modo decisamente positivo in Eredivisie, ma è reduce da due pareggi nelle ultime due uscite in campionato: dopo l'1-1 in casa del PSV Eindhoven è arrivato quello interno contro il Fortuna Sittard, con il gol del pari ospite arrivato a cinque minuti dalla fine. Diverso il rendimento in Champions League, dove gli uomini di Van Bronckhorst hanno raccolto tre punti in tre partite, frutto dei k.o. esterni con Barcellona e Betis e il successo casalingo contro il Como.

Nel match contro la squadra di Fabregas, giocato davanti al proprio pubblico, gli olandesi sono stati trascinati dalle giocate di Moussa e di Zechiel. Il primo, ala destra veloce e abile nell'uno contro uno, ama convergere sul sinistro e cercare la conclusione: proprio con un'azione del genere ha innescato il terzo gol del Feyenoord, con il suo mancino deviato che è entrato in porta dopo l'ultimo tocco di Sanchez.

Il secondo, invece, è un centrocampista dinamico dagli ottimi tempi di inserimento, qualità nello stretto e capacità balistica: abilità che ha messo in mostra in occasione della rete del pari contro il Como, girandosi in un fazzoletto e concludendo dal limite dell'area.

Testata giornalistica registrata - Aut.Trib.di Bologna n. 8183 del 17/05/2011 AMALA S.N.C. - Direttore Responsabile: Daniele Mari e Giovanni Montopoli - Vice Direttore : Sabine Bertagna Il sito FCINTER1908 di titolarità di AMALA S.N.C. con sede in Bologna Via del Pratello, 27 C.F./PI e numero di iscrizione al Reg. #2307. Landucci a DAZN: “Abbiamo fatto un brutto secondo tempo. Gli infortuni non sono una scusante”

Marco Landucci, vice allenatore del Napoli, ha rilasciato alcune dichiarazioni a DAZN, dopo la sconfitta contro la Juventus: “Abbiamo fatto un brutto secondo tempo, la Juventus ha meritato di vincere. Ci sono stati degli infortuni, ma non è una scusante, dovevamo fare meglio, dopo un buon primo tempo. Preoccupati no, ma siamo molto dispiaciuti, la partita è stata questa, ora stiamo zitti e prepariamo la partita per mercoledì. Non solo noi abbiamo infortuni, c’è da parlare poco e da fare. Siamo scappati troppo e abbiamo preso gol, è la prima volta che lo rivedo ed è stato fatto un gran gol, se esci bene non prendi gol. Di Lorenzo, De Bruyne e Olivera ha avuto un problema alla caviglia, gli altri due non lo so ma non potevano continuare, Kevin ha avuto una storta, noi però dobbiamo fare meglio nel secondo tempo, c’è poco da parlare, loro hanno fatto meglio di noi e hanno meritato la vittoria abbiamo pagato gli errori”.

I Am Naples Testata Giornalistica - aut. #2306. Pioli: “Ci sono stati momenti in cui mi hanno chiamato tutti. Anche la Roma”

L’ex allenatore della Fiorentina racconta del motivo per cui andò ad allenare l’Al Nassr e di quando fu chiamato dalla Roma e dalla Nazionale

Stefano Pioli ex allenatore biancoceleste ha legato una delle pagine più esaltanti della sua carriera proprio alla panchina della Lazio, condotta nel 2014-2015 a uno straordinario terzo posto e a una finale di Coppa Italia persa contro la Juve. L’apice della sua carriera il tecnico lo  ha raggiunto con lo storico scudetto vinto alla guida del Milan nel 2022. Il tecnico è stato  intervistato per Sky Sport, raccontando di quando fu vicino alla Roma e alla Nazionale.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Ci sono stati momenti in cui mi hanno chiamato tutti. Anche la Roma, ma mi era appena arrivato il contratto dell’Al Nassr, ma non mi sono pentito di andare in Arabia. Volevo un’esperienza fuori. Hanno inciso l’aspetto economico e la possibilità di allenare Cristiano Ronaldo. Anche la panchina della Nazionale l’ho sfiorata e non da moltissimo.

Nell’estate del 2025 Pioli torna a Firenze dopo esserci stato sia da allenatore tra 1989-95 che da allenatore tra il 2017-19. Purtroppo per il tecnico la sua seconda esperienza nella Fiorentina si è rivelata estremamente negativa: nelle prime 10 partite di campionato la squadra non ha mai vinto, raccogliendo appena 4 pareggi, subendo 6 sconfitte e totalizzando solo 4 punti. Un avvio da record negativo che ha portato inevitabilmente all'esonero il 4 novembre 2025.
